That near-miss I had really set it off. Since then, anytime I’m at the clinic or even thinking about being a nurse, I start feeling like I’m dangerous somehow, like I’m going to hurt someone. And then it turns into feeling like I’m just a disappointment, like maybe I’m not meant to do this and people would be better off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period, no matter how brief, where these feelings of being a danger, a disappointment, and the loss of interest in activities significantly improved over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It’s been going on since after I graduated high school and started college and working full-time at the clinic, so about a year and two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations, people, or events that make you feel more depressed or trigger these feelings of being a danger and a disappointment?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s mostly work-related. That near-miss I had really set it off. Since then, anytime I’m at the clinic or even thinking about being a nurse, I start feeling like I’m dangerous somehow, like I’m going to hurt someone. And then it turns into feeling like I’m just a disappointment, like maybe I’m not meant to do this and people would be better off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period, no matter how brief, where these feelings of being a danger, a disappointment, and the loss of interest in activities significantly improved over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It’s been pretty steady since after I graduated high school and started college classes and working full-time at the clinic… so, about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or events outside of work that make you feel more depressed or trigger these feelings of being a danger and a disappointment?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, not really anything specific. It mostly comes back to that near-miss and what it makes me think about myself. Even when I’m not at the clinic, I’ll remember it and start feeling like it proves I’m dangerous, like I’m a disappointment and maybe I shouldn’t be a nurse.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two months where you felt significantly better and your depressive symptoms were much less bothersome?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. It’s been going on pretty consistently for about a year and two months, since I finished high school and started college classes and working full-time at the clinic. I haven’t really had a two-month stretch where it felt much better.\n\nDoctor: Has your current episode of depression lasted for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No, not more than two years. It’s been about a year and two months, since after I graduated high school and started college classes and working full-time at the clinic.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of depression like this in your lifetime?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve had another time like this before, at least not that I can clearly point to. This feels like the main time it’s been this bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
